Barnsley FC and Daniel Stendel welcomed fun back like an old friend in FA Cup win v Aldershot Town - analysis

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

As Daniel Stendel sat down to dissect Barnsley’s 7-2 FA Cup win over Aldershot Town, he offered a wry smile.

He had been asked about the two goals his side leaked late in a game that had already been taken well beyond the opposition.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

It was a question he will have known was coming, and a question most managers would have enjoyed answering to demonstrate their forensic and demanding nature. The word ‘standards’ would have most likely been used.

Stendel, however, had no interest in poking holes in a mostly scintillating performance. And who could blame him?

Barnsley’s campaign has been largely devoid of fun and rife with simmering discontent. With Bonfire Night celebrations spilling over into the weekend, Stendel clearly felt the Reds needed to simply enjoy the sparks.

“We keep the positive things,” said Stendel. “It's normal that I think about the two goals, but I think we analysed a lot in the last couple of weeks about some situations, some games, and why we didn't win the game in the end.

“I think today is time to say ‘good result, we're in the hat for the next draw’.”

Barnsley flew out of the traps at a dizzying pace and the brakes were simply never applied. By half-time, Cameron McGeehan and Vimal Yoganathan braces and a Reyes Cleary effort had Aldershot dead and buried.

McGeehan completed his hat-trick after the break and Rylan Brownlie got in the act, with the latter rounding off the afternoon after Aldershot had twice hit back via Annisuis Lewis.

“It was good to see that we used the spaces that we get, and a goal after a set-piece.

“I think three goals after crosses was good, especially that. It's one thing that we try to change and what we train in training, why we said we have to cross more balls in the box. And yeah, it was good today.”

Toxicity has enveloped Oakwell in recent months and while it has not been Stendel’s doing, it is clear he is relishing the trickling return of some positivity.

His side’s hard-fought draw with Oxford United a week prior evoked nostalgia, memories of a first spell in which supporter connection was strong.

They did not require the same spirit against the Shots but bluntly, it was just nice to see some goalscoring fun. It is a pleasure with which the Oakwell faithful have not been well-acquainted of late.

“We needed this game,” said Stendel. “It's a good opportunity for everybody to prove what we want to do next week and to get some confidence in defence and offence for winning duels, try to do things, cross the ball early, and in the box you can score goals.

“We have tough weekends in the past, and I think it was good for everybody, for the mindset, for the training time next week, for the game, especially before the weekend game.”

Barnsley will host Oldham Athletic in the Jersey Mike’s Trophy on Wednesday (November 11) but it is clear Stendel’s primary focus is on the Brick Community Stadium. That is where Wigan Athletic will entertain the Reds in a battle of two of League One’s bottom four sides on Saturday (November 14).

A battling league draw followed by a thorough dismantling of an inferior FA Cup opponent represents a start - but it would take a league win for the phrase ‘turning point’ to be uttered with any conviction. At present, it can only be whispered.

“I think we play sometimes really well, but we get no points for that,” said Stendel. “You have to score, you have to defend.

“I think it's important that everybody looks for situations that you can score, not only our strikers.

“I think this is important, especially for the next weeks when we have some games against teams around our situation in the table, but also we talk a lot about our defence behaviour, we have to improve that, and I think it was a good step in the right direction today.”

Barnsley: Slicker, Okedina, Gale (Noslin 76), Kay, Larkeche; Johnson (Kelly 63), Connell; Yoganathan, McGeehan (Taylor 71), Cleary (Brownlie 63); Adeyemo (Kirk 45).

Unused substitutes: Ravenhill, Smith, Richards, Alker.

Aldershot Town: Hayes, Dyce (Robinson 60), Oshilaja, Stickland, Harfield (Adom-Malaki 60); Borgnis, Odusanya; Peart (Gilbert 72), Tima, Sandah (Lewis-Evans 78); Abrahams (Lewis 60).

Unused substitutes: Patmore, Matton, Wyllie, Dinanga.

Marketers Are BURNED OUT - And No One Gives AF

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I have read countless posts about bloated marketing roles where six jobs are crammed into one title. One listing asked for a “Marketing Manager” to handle brand strategy, paid media, SEO, email marketing, copywriting, and front-end web design. All of that... on a junior salary! These are not rare. They are the norm these days. Scroll LinkedIn or Reddit for five minutes and you will see post after post of people venting about being stretched thin, underpaid, and told to be “grateful for the opportunity.”

I have been reading these for a YEAR now. Watching the same story play out. Marketers are asking, “Is it just me, or has marketing completely lost the plot? Is this the job I wanted? Should I just switch careers?” I even asked the same things myself. I used to love marketing. Now, I don't even know what to say.

What even was marketing before all of this? Was it always like this and we are just noticing it now? Or did it get worse in the last few years?

What used to be a blend of strategy and execution turned into survival mode. AI promised efficiency. Instead, it created more expectation. Faster turnarounds. More output. Tighter budgets. Leadership sees AI as a shortcut, not a tool. So the workload? It went up. Headcount? Went down. Pressure? TRIPLED.

The marketer role did not evolve. It got gutted and repackaged.

This article is not about the trends. Not the hype. Just focusing on the one question we all dread to be answered. How real is marketer burnout, and how did we get here?

What happens when your creative energy gets consumed by meetings, client briefs, and endless edits? How do you stay motivated when everyone blames your team for poor performance but no one credits you when a campaign wins? Why do marketers, especially in digital, feel like they are always running on empty?

Burnout in marketing is not new. What’s different now is how fast it spreads and how deeply it affects people across roles. From content specialists and media buyers to strategists and SEO managers, burnout is everywhere. Not because people are lazy or bad at their jobs. They are overwhelmed, underappreciated, and stuck in systems that demand more while offering less.

Marketers today wear too many hats. Generalist roles stretch across brand, analytics, operations, and sales support. It used to be enough to run great creative. Now you are expected to prove ROI, optimize in real-time, adjust to AI tools, and still produce on-brand messaging across ten platforms. The second something underperforms, marketing takes the fall.

But when results are good? Sales or other department will take the credit. That’s not an exaggeration. One Reddit comment I saw earlier hit it perfectly: “The sales team shouldn't be the only ones getting commission since we (content team) did all the work.” That’s the norm in many agencies and in-house teams.

Marketers drive interest, create value, and nurture leads. Sales closes once the hard part is already done.

There’s a word for that dynamic: exhausting.

Burnout is tied to more than just being busy. It’s the layoffs. The increased cost of living. The consolidation of roles. Managers freeze raises. Employees fear losing their jobs, so they overperform to avoid the next round of cuts. That creates a cycle. You take on more. You work harder. You don’t complain. And then your reward is more responsibility, not more support. Not a better salary.

You become your own bottleneck. You say yes to everything because the team is small and leadership needs “just one more push.” Eventually, you hit a wall. Creativity dies. Enthusiasm fades. Work turns into survival.

Marketing roles are highly visible but rarely respected. Everyone thinks they can do your job. Leadership gives vague direction but expects strong positioning. Product teams shift priorities mid-sprint. Sales wants new decks with impossible turnaround times. People expect you to spin gold out of nonsense with a smile on your face.

Meanwhile, your inbox fills up with subject lines like “quick tweak,” “minor edit,” and “urgent request.” These aren’t minor. They’re entire projects disguised as favors. And they never stop.

This is why boundaries matter. The problem is, setting boundaries gets seen as being “difficult” or “not a team player.” So people stop trying. They take the hits in silence.

A common theme in marketing burnout is the rejection of sales culture. Some marketers say, “I don’t want to do sales. I’m an introvert.” That mindset makes you easy to ignore in corporate environments. One Reddit commenter put it plainly. Marketing is sales. You sell ideas to bosses, value props to clients, and narratives to users. You’re not on the phone closing deals, but you are pitching all the time.

People who thrive in marketing know how to talk. They present data with confidence. They make their case without waiting for approval. They build allies. And yes, sometimes they party with clients to keep them loyal.

The problem is not that marketers don’t work hard. It’s that most of them don’t get taught how to sell themselves. That disconnect builds resentment over time.

Fixing burnout in marketing requires more than taking a week off or downloading a mindfulness app. Companies and leadership need structural change.

1. Credit needs to be shared. If a campaign brings in leads, content, media, and analytics should all get credit. This is not a favor. It’s the truth! Stop handing out bonuses to sales without acknowledging who did the heavy lifting.

2. Boundaries must be normalized. No one should work weekends to chase someone else’s last-minute idea. Make deadlines real. Protect personal time. Normalize saying no.

3. Redefine performance. Measuring success only through ROI creates a narrow path to burnout. Not every channel performs the same way. Not every campaign wins in thirty days. Look at long-term impact, brand growth, and engagement quality.

4. Advocate up the chain. Teach marketers how to pitch. Not just to customers. To leadership. Make the case for headcount, for tools, for strategy. If you don’t make noise, you won’t get noticed.

5. Say the quiet part out loud. Marketers need space to admit when they are tired. When they feel ignored. When they need clarity. Create a culture where people can be honest without fear.

Burnout doesn’t mean you are in the wrong field. It means the structure around you is broken. You shouldn’t need to chase credit. You shouldn’t need to hide exhaustion. You shouldn’t be constantly afraid of layoffs or budget cuts or being replaced by automation.

Good marketing is emotional work. It requires A LOT of creativity, patience, and most importantly clarity. You cannot fake that for long. At some point, your output suffers and your health goes with it.

There is a way forward, but it starts with truth. Stop pretending everything’s fine. Start fixing the real problems.

If your team is burned out, they don’t need a pep talk. They need help. Give them that, or lose them.

Wrote this for every marketer who’s been holding it in.

Neuroscientists say people can be "trained" to recognize when they're dreaming — in other words, lucid dreaming may be an acquirable skill.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

During dreams, bizarre scenes you encounter can seem as tangible as reality; you feel as though you're really flying through the sky or being chased through a jungle of bright-purple trees. But in special cases, people realize they're in the midst of a dream as it's unfolding.

Called lucid dreaming, this phenomenon occurs when we suddenly regain our capacity for metacognition, or thinking about our own thoughts, in the middle of a dream. Lucid dreamers can sometimes even control the content or trajectory of their dreams. Now, researchers claim that they've found a way to "train" people to lucid dream by having them meditate and complete virtual-reality-augmented exercises while they're awake.

In a study involving a few dozen participants, the scientists found that repeating this training twice a week for a month significantly increased how often participants experienced lucid dreaming. They say the approach could have practical applications as an intervention for people with nightmares, anxiety or post-traumatic stress disorder — but those applications are theoretical, for now.

If you could learn how to lucid dream, would you? Let us know in the poll below, and add your thoughts in the comments.

Live Tinted’s Founder Shares Her Secret to a "Luminous" Diwali Glow

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

E! exclusively spoke with Deepica Mutyala, founder of Live Tinted, about how to achieve a "luminous" glow for Diwali, and how she formulates her unique & purposeful products.

Live Tinted made history just over 5 years ago at Ulta Beauty when they became the first South Asian-founded makeup brand to launch there, and recently came together for a special Diwali celebration.

Following her star-studded event, E! spoke exclusively with founder Deepica Mutyala about her partnership with Ulta, the best products to get a luminous Diwali glow, plus her commitment to purposeful formulas and inclusive shade ranges.

Deepica shared, "It's honestly such a full-circle moment! When we launched at Ulta Beauty in 2021 as its first South Asian-founded makeup brand, it was a milestone I never could have imagined growing up. Five years later, we're one of Ulta's fastest-growing prestige cosmetics brands!"

"Hosting one of the beauty industry's largest Diwali celebrations alongside Ulta's leadership this year was incredibly meaningful. Seeing nearly 200 people from across beauty, fashion, and entertainment come together to celebrate my culture reminded me just how far we've come and what's possible for the next generation of founders," she added.

What 3 products would you recommend for a luminous glow for Diwali?

"I actually wore all three of these to our Diwali celebration with Ulta Beauty! I started with our HUEGUARD Skin Tint SPF 50 for a beautiful, even complexion, added HUEGLOW for that gorgeous radiance, and finished with HUEBEAM Gradient Blushing Bronzer for warmth and dimension. It's my favorite combination for a fresh, luminous look that still feels like your skin!"

The brand's bestselling Hueguard Skin Tint offers buildable, light-to-medium coverage made with skincare actives.

For anyone new to the brand, Deepica says that the bestselling Hueguard ("one sells every two minutes globally" at Ulta, she adds) is what she'd recommend trying out first: "I love it because it combines skincare, SPF 50 protection, and beautiful, natural-looking coverage in one easy step. It's the perfect everyday product and really represents our mission of creating beauty that works for everyone, from the lightest to the deepest skin tones!"

Subtle microshimmers give your skin a naturally radiant and hydrated glow in three different colors: Dawn (rose gold), Dusk (bronze) and Golden Hour (soft gold).

This highly-pigmented gradient bronzer provides a perfectly sunkissed glow, made with hyaluronic acid for hydration and niacinamide to even skin tone.

Of the HUEBEAM Gradient Blushing Bronzer, Deepica added, "I love that it gives you warmth and a beautiful flush of color in one product. My favorite tip is to swirl your brush across the gradient for a seamless, sun-kissed glow, or focus on individual shades to customize your look. Apply it to the high points of your cheeks and blend upward for a naturally lifted finish!"

"That glow comes from feeling happy, confident, and surrounded by the people I love! Diwali has always been such a special time to celebrate family, culture, and new beginnings. Hosting our Diwali celebration with Ulta Beauty this year and seeing so many people from different backgrounds come together was such a beautiful reminder of what makes this holiday so meaningful to me! But if we're talking about an external glow, it has to be our HUEGLOW Liquid Highlighter Drops for that gorgeous, lit-from-within radiance!"

The perfect set to gift or keep for yourself, it includes the trio of HUEGLOW shades: Dawn, Golden Hour and Dusk.

What is your product development process like, especially when creating products to work for a wide range of shades?

"Everything starts with listening to our community and identifying real gaps in the beauty industry. Live Tinted was created for everyone, from light to the deep skin tones, and that philosophy guides every product we develop. We're incredibly intentional about shades, undertones, formulas, and how products perform across different complexions. Our goal is to create thoughtful, innovative products that make beauty easier and help everyone feel seen!"

This skincare-infused concealer has a serum-like texture, proviidng medium-to-full coverage while helping to improve the look of fine lines.

This complexion stick offers medium-to-full coverage with a blurred, soft-matte finish. Available in a range of shades, it still feels lightweight on the skin thanks to the hydrating skincare ingredients.

How do you recommend using the Color Corrector?

"My biggest tip is to start with less than you think you need! Apply a small amount of HUESTICK Color Corrector to areas of discoloration, like under the eyes or around the mouth, and gently blend before applying your foundation or concealer. It comes in five shades designed to address different skin tones and discoloration concerns, so there's an option for everyone! The goal is to neutralize discoloration rather than cover it with layers of makeup. Color correcting is actually what started my entire journey and ultimately inspired me to create Live Tinted!"

Available in five different shades, with options for varying concerns and skin tones, this creamy multistick features hydrating skincare ingredients for a smooth and blendable finish.

5 unexpected uses for your old smartwatch

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Breath new life into your old tech with these handy tips.

Upgrading to one of the best smartwatches can be exciting, but it often means your old smartwatch is left with nothing to do. Unfortunately, it's at this point that users may toss their old smartwatch in a drawer and forget about it. But it doesn't have to be like that.

Your old smartwatch is a versatile piece of hardware, so despite how it seems, that small device that's been gathering dust still has some pretty unexpected and interesting uses. With the right guidance, you can give your old smartwatch a new lease on life, or at the very least, you could sell it for some quick cash. Even if your smartwatch no longer receives major OS updates, you can still use it as a dedicated AI tool, an easy-access note-taking device, a child monitoring tool, or a dedicated sleep tracker. So free your old smartwatch from its drawer and blow the dust off. Most of these unexpected uses only take a few minutes to set up and require an extra app or two, though some require a cellular plan or a phone for full functionality.

If you use AI tools like Gemini or ChatGPT, one of the more relevant uses for your old smartwatch is to turn it into a dedicated AI assistant device. Setting things up is pretty simple, allowing you to get answers from or talk to your AI, all without having to pull out your phone. On Wear OS smartwatches, Gemini is the primary option. While on the Apple Watch, Siri is the default — but you don't have to stick with Siri.

On watches running Wear OS 4 or later, you'll need to first make sure Gemini is installed and has been updated. Once that's sorted and voice activation is enabled, all you need to do is say "Hey Google," and Gemini will respond. If voice activation isn't your style, you can alternatively press and hold the side button to activate Gemini. On Samsung watches, you'll need to customize the side button so it can activate Gemini. To reassign, open Settings > Button and gestures > Double press > scroll to where you see Gemini and select it.

On the Apple Watch, activating Siri is straightforward; simply raise your wrist and say "Hey Siri" or press and hold the Digital Crown. You can use the Wrist AI app to gain ChatGPT or Gemini functionality, but the free tier only allows a small number of chats daily. For easier access, you can add a Wrist AI complication to your watch face. For Wear OS watches, to get ChatGPT going, you'll need the WristAssist app, but it's been removed from the Play Store, so you will have to sideload it. You'll also need an API key from OpenAI to get things going; creating the key is free, but usage is billed.

A good use case for your old smartwatch is simply making it a note-taking device thanks to more accurate AI transcriptions. So instead of having to pull out your phone from your pocket every time you get an idea or want to jot down some thoughts, you can raise your wrist and speak.

Your old smartwatch likely already has a note or voice recorder app, so what you need is preinstalled. On Apple Watch, you can say "Hey Siri," then "Record a voice memo" to get started.

You can get transcriptions of the notes recorded on both Wear OS watches and Apple Watch, but you'll need your phone. On Samsung watches, you can get transcriptions in the voice recorder app using the built-in speech-to-text mode, but it has a 10-minute limit. Recordings taken on your smartwatch should automatically sync across your devices, so a note you recorded during your morning walk should be waiting on your phone by the time you get home.

There are few things as important to the human body as sleep, and your old smartwatch can help you improve it. But you'll have to sacrifice charging your watch overnight, potentially leaving you with a drained battery in the morning. And that's precisely where your old watch comes in. You can charge it all day, then wear it to bed while your main smartwatch charges.

Setup usually takes just a few minutes. To track your sleep and view your sleep data on Apple Watch, set up Sleep in the Health app on your iPhone, then turn on the Track Sleep with Apple Watch setting. For a Galaxy Watch, connect the watch to your phone, then open the Sleep tile on your Galaxy Watch and follow the prompts to get it set up. Keep in mind that how well this works and how much data it can track depends on the model of your smartwatch, because they don't all offer the same sensors, especially older models.

Lastly, don't treat the reported numbers with the same weight as a diagnosis from your doctor, because sensors can be inaccurate. So, if you notice anything concerning in your sleep report, take it as a cue to talk to your doctor, not as a diagnosis.

Your old smartwatch can find a new purpose on your child's wrist as a dedicated kids' watch. When set up, your kids can call, text, and share locations without owning a smartphone. Do note that this only works on LTE/Cellular smartwatches, and a smartphone isn't needed for the child after configuration.

Samsung watches have a dedicated Kids mode that gives parents plenty of oversight over their child's experience. So apart from the usual location monitoring, calls, and texts, parents can also approve or block apps, set School Time limits, and use Google Family Link to manage the child's watch usage. Unfortunately, Kids mode isn't available on every Samsung Watch; you'll need an LTE Galaxy Watch 6 and above.

Apple Watch offers a similar feature called Apple Watch for Your Kids, and it's supported on Series 4 or later with cellular, or an SE with cellular on watchOS 7 or later. As you'd expect, it can make calls and send texts, has location tracking, screen time management, and Schooltime mode, which, like Samsung's equivalent, is basically restricted so the child doesn't get distracted during school hours.

Keep in mind that cellular features require a carrier plan, which means additional fees for you. And carrier support varies, so some carriers may not support standalone calling from a smartwatch.

If none of the uses work for you, or you'd just prefer to get rid of your device, then your best option is to sell your old smartwatch. It probably still holds some resale value; it's only a matter of finding the right place (or way) to sell it. There are two main routes to go with.

First, you can sell on marketplaces like eBay. Selling on a marketplace may give you the highest payout, but it also comes with the most hassles. You'll have to handle everything concerning the sale, including writing an accurate listing, taking clear pictures, setting a good price, answering questions from window shoppers, shipping it, and then calculating how much platform fees cost you.

Selling to a buyback company like Greenbuyback is the second option that will likely net you less than a marketplace, but it can be more convenient. Simply get a quote online, then ship your old smartwatch to the buyer. Once it's received, it'll be inspected, and if the condition you described matches, you get paid. If it doesn't, you can receive a revised offer. How long the whole process takes depends on the specific program; some pay in as little as 24 business hours after receiving your watch, while others can take up to five business days.

If you don't mind getting store credit and less value for your old smartwatch, then you can trade in your old smartwatch to retailers like Best Buy or manufacturer programs like Apple Trade In. Before you sell or trade in your old smartwatch, make sure to unpair it from your phone and wipe it. On an Apple Watch, unpairing erases it. On Wear OS, sign out of your accounts and factory reset it.

Gemini North reveals Twin Spirits in Pegasus

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Three high school students in the Project Hōkūlani internship at Gemini North on Maunakea, Hawaii, chose this pair of clashing spirals, cataloged collectively as NGC 7253 or Arp 278, for imaging with the 8.1-meter telescope. It captured this photo — the most detailed ever taken of these galaxies — in October 2025. The students selected this target because it’s in a traditional Hawaiian constellation, Ka Lupe o Kawelo, or the Kite of Kawelo, located in Pegasus. In June 2026, three more interns gave the pair of galaxies a new name: Nā ʻUhane Māhoe Huki Pū i ke Ola, meaning “the Twin Spirits Pulling Together Creating Life” in Hawaiian. The title refers to the way that, over billions of years, the two galaxies will merge, sparking the formation of new stars and planets.

Alison Klesman is senior editor of Astronomy magazine. She holds a Ph.D. in astronomy and has studied a variety of topics, from minor planets to supermassive black holes.

Meet the 27-Year-Old Who’s Trying To Bring Housing Back to Rural Ohio

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

When it comes to the hottest locations that attract young 20-somethings, Mansfield, OH, probably isn't the first place that comes to mind.

For years, Mansfield’s nickname was “The Fun Center of Ohio” because of its family-friendly attractions and its status in the 1970s as a mini-bike manufacturing hub.

More recently, the town of 47,000, located halfway between Columbus and Cleveland, earned the local nickname of “Danger City” after the Westinghouse electric plant left in 1990 and the town fell into deep economic decline.

The 27-year-old serial entrepreneur, who started a painting and landscaping business as a teenager, relocated to Mansfield from Seattle a year ago, hoping to revitalize rural housing through his vertically integrated housing development company, Western Dominion Land.

Vertical integration is a development model in which a single company controls the full life cycle of a housing project.

“In other countries, they’ve figured out that vertically integrating construction means you’re not paying a margin out to every single step," Goehner explains. "If you own the system—from a lumberyard, to producing panels or modular, to buying the land, to developing the land, to building the homes—you're not paying everyone’s markup along the way, and you can actually deliver that home at a reasonable price.”

Goehner is building a factory in Mansfield to produce structured panels that he says will cut single-family home construction from months to weeks and allow him to charge much less than other builders. The first panels are expected to roll off the production line in early 2027.

Currently, it takes 8.8 months to construct a home from start to finish, according to the National Association of Home Builders. Goehner says his process allows a home to be built and move-in-ready in 40 days.

Western Dominion has already built about 500 housing units across Ohio, starting at $200,000 for a single-family home.

Western Dominion focuses on what it calls "attainable housing"—homes priced below what the median household income in the area can afford but not income-limited, meaning they work for the vast majority of families and buyers.

In Mansfield, where the median listing price is $229,000, he says, "single-family homes at $215,000 sold instantly. At $260,000, they sat for months."

As he sees it, 25% of the homes on the market are priced for 60% to 70% of buyers, while the rest of what’s being built is at the top end of the market. Furthermore, only 8% of construction happens in rural areas, despite 20% of the population living there.

"The government is too focused on low-income housing" and low-income housing tax credits, he says. “And the last few housing bills they passed were only structured to deliver essentially more urban multifamily units and incentivize builders to build more low-income housing with tax credits."

In rural communities, he adds, “We have a terrible amount of aged housing inventory. Most of our homes are from before 1970, which would be fine if it didn't cost so much to remodel.

“From the '90s to the 2000s, we’re building a good amount of homes. As a country, we were doing about 1.6 million a year. And then in rural America, we were doing about 220,000 homes a year. And then 2008 hits, and that drops down to about 60,000 homes a year in rural America."

Along the way, smaller builders disappeared, and larger builders were disincentivized from building in rural areas because of the red tape involved.

"If you want to build in rural America and you go to a bank, unless you have all the cash, you have to show comps," he says. "You've got to be, like, ‘All right, we think these homes are going to sell for this much because these guys sold for this much.' And if no one's built for five to 50 years, which is the case in a lot of these communities, that can be difficult."

The added costs of building in rural areas, like having to pull utilities, "mean you're spending $25,000 to $50,000 per lot before you can even start putting a house on it or connecting your utilities to the lot."

Since the COVID-19 pandemic, home prices in rural communities have risen sharply. A Realtor.com® housing analysis of home prices from November 2019 to November 2025 found that median listing prices increased more than 70% in rural counties while in-metro counties saw home prices increase around 30% during the same time period.

"A lot of the politicians we talk to, they go, 'I didn't know that that county needed housing. I didn't know rural needed housing because the population is shrinking.' But then when you look at why the population is shrinking, it's because they haven't gotten any new homes for 20 to 40 years."

"You've got this huge demand. But on paper, on the surface, it looks like there's no demand because the population's declining," he continues. "And that's how you get cities like Holiday City."

Holiday City, near the Ohio border with Indiana and Michigan, has a population of just 48 people, but it is home to three large businesses—Chase Brass & Copper Co., 20/20 Custom Molded Products, and a Menards Distribution Center—which draw around 1,500 commuters from out of state.

"Let's put up, you know, 300 homes, and a portion of the workforce can actually stop commuting an hour or two every day," Goehner says.

Goehner hopes Ohio's new slate of elected officials will help advance rural building by offsetting some of the infrastructure-related costs of building in rural areas. In exchange, he would sell homes at his “attainable” housing rates.

"The government needs to stop spending thousands of dollars a month per unit to subsidize rents when they could simply spend a little bit upfront to make it viable to build at a reasonable price point again," he says.

Julie Gerstein is a writer and editor based in Philadelphia. She previously worked at Business Insider as an executive editor covering news, entertainment, and digital culture, and was the company's founding Singapore bureau chief. She was also a senior editor at BuzzFeed, covering lifestyle, fashion, and beauty. She earned a B.A. in women's studies from the University of Pennsylvania and an M.A. in media and cultural studies from the University of Sussex.

Fan injured after falling through hole in stand

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Tromso's Romssa Arena, pictured before a Conference League game against Brighton in August, is 200 miles inside the Arctic Circle

A fan was taken to hospital after a number of Bodo/Glimt supporters fell through a hole in the away stand at Tromso.

The Norwegian top-flight match at Romssa Arena on Sunday was paused just before half-time after Bodo keeper Nikita Haikin called for the referee's attention.

Bodo/Glimt press officer Simen Pedersen told the Reuters news agency that "up to five people fell through the stands".

"We stood and bounced to a supporter song, then the floor gave way," Bodo supporter Andrine Moen told local newspaper Verdens Gang, as reported by Reuters.

"It cracked in two in the row in front of us. There were at least two people who fell through the floor, but we don't know how far they fell or how many fell in total."

The remaining Bodo supporters were moved into other parts of the ground before the match resumed.

The match at Tromso's stadium - the northernmost venue in Norway's Eliteserien - was played in heavy rain.

The score was 0-0 at the time of the incident, but Bodo forward Ola Brynhildsen scored twice after the game resumed to give the league leaders a 2-0 win.

Bodo are locked in a title battle with Viking and the Eliteserien season is set to finish in the first week of December.

De Rossi: "Ho detto a Modric che non lo posso più menare. Amorim? Lo studio e mi piace come comunica"

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Speedrunners Are Already Breaking The Legend Of Zelda: Ocarina Of Time Remake In Absurd Ways

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

NintendoSpeedrunningThe Legend of ZeldaThe Legend of Zelda: Ocarina Of Time

The anticipated remake of the seminal Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time dropped this week. Lifelong fans are returning to a familiar but altered Hyrule. The world originally rendered in 1998 has greater fidelity, hunkier non-playable characters, wiser chickens and, most notably, a jump button. For the regular player itâs a marvel and an adjustment. For speedrunners, itâs a tantalizing playground of tech to be discovered. As the weekend rolls out, and runners dredge the kingdom for secret exploits, theyâre establishing a potential blueprint for record times. As well as uncovering some bizarre horrors along the way.

To say The Legend of Zelda series has some abnormal tech is an understatement. Ever since Link stepped out of his treehouse into a 3D, polygonal world, it was clear the code that binds his reality had a soft underbelly. Ocarinaâs boomerang can rewrite item placement. Wind Wakerâs camera can launch little Toon Link into hyperspeed across the ocean. Skyward Swordâs opening menu seems to exist in some kind of temporal pocket dimension. Nintendoâs fine tuned games have always operated on peculiar physics, but for speedrunners, a return to Ocarina presents an opportunity to rewrite the playbook.

Ladies and gentlemen, we have a speedrun pic.twitter.com/hRN97USavV

The most obvious source for potential run-aiding glitches is Linkâs newfound ability to jump. Like in Breath of the Wild, Link no longer needs to run off a cliff to remember how to use his knees. In standard play, Link can go higher. Speedrunners want to see just how high he can go. Find unassuming geography that can act as shortcuts. Some of the classics, like the building-bounding ‘Hookshot Jump,’ appear to be viable. One of the most popular pieces of tech revolves around open chests. Now that Link can step into them, positioning his feet inside them at just the right angle seems to bristle the game, launching the player like a trampoline. Dropping a bomb in there seems to help as well.

Like previous Zelda games, plenty of glitches lay dormant outside of realtime play. Remake runners are already stress testing start menus and inventory to see what will happen when spammed and agitated. And sure enough, flipping between items in your inventory midair seems to cause Link to float like a leaf in the wind.

“This bug shouldn’t exist lol,” posted ORCinfo. “Once you get used to it, you can jump endlessly in midair. This is gonna break speedruns.”

Defying gravity is obviously useful, but potential sequence breaking exploits is what plummets a runtime from hours into minutes. Stumbling across the proper tech could allow players to skip items, temples, plot beats or reroute them into a more convenient order. This is especially prescient in a game that swaps between two different periods. As runner Zfg1 is chronicling, messing with the hero of time does seem to have a bit of a butterfly effect. While sadly temporary, triggering cut adult Link cutscenes as child Link, and vice versa, momentarily confuses the remake, scrunching his texture files into a neanderthal, or the product of coming out the other end of Amigara Fault.

We can now beat Shadow Temple as child, which automatically turns you into adult. We still have 1 roadblock to get past before this can be used in a speedrun, but it'll be huge assuming we can get past it.

It also looks very funny. pic.twitter.com/s2aoTKCQyY

The day is young as far as how any of this can be applied. Glitches can be awe inspiring, but if they donât contribute to faster runs theyâre just a party trick. Likewise seemingly benign exploits can sometimes pierce through to monumental discoveries, especially as run conditions become more niche. But things are moving fast, even for a scene preoccupied with being fast. The attention to a speedrunning classic means new glitches will be hitting hard and fast. And Link, poor Link, is along for the ride.

Vitalik Buterin Declares Ethereum (ETH) a Store of Value, Not Fee-Burning Token

📅 2026-11-08  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ethereum's intrinsic economics is rebuilding itself in motion with tech bugs and the macroeconomic picture overlaying each other in the span of 48 hours. The week wraps up for a major alternative crypto project with a sudden Glamsterdam hard fork stop after testnet launch revealed a dangerous bug in the fee recalculating mechanism, brutally breaking the logic in smart contracts.

As the Ethereum community debated the ability of the network to sustain on old power, Vitalik Buterin showed up with a manifesto which completely zeroes out all the panic around mainnet's decreasing revenue. The "red line" behind Vitalik's updated vision is that the community should stop being fixated on the idea of ETH as "fee-burninng token". For a basic blockchain layer worth $270 billion, attempts to build evaluation on the amount of gas burned are a dead end. For Vitalik, the novel of "ETH as store of value (SoV)" is much more viable amid all the global uncertainty around.

This U-turn, taken by a key visionary of the ecosystem, happened at $2,240 for ETH and may drastically change all the plans developed with a focus on Glamsterdam architecture. The hard fork was initially projected for the logic of blockspace surplus, not the deficit. Ecosystem builders are already readying expansion of L1 capacity with the help of EIP-8037 (tariffs calibration) and EIP-7928 (parallel production) with an aim to raise gas limits up to 200 million units. The logic is that the excess of free space will cheapen the transactions, while token burning rates will gradually be cut.

The market saw a risk of deflation in this concept. Vitalik took a front-step. By his estimates, the historical peaks of burning through EIP-1559 "never were as strong as they seemed". They were dependent on inner cyclical activity of crypto speculations. And it itself was falling once every bear market started.

Attempts to squeeze the premium out of the protocol by any means would push the system toward overly aggressive management and interventions, says Buterin. Ethereum would be required to become a centralized commercial project with inflated costs on research and development in other words. What the market needs meanwhile, amid the rise of AI and regulatory storms, is not a "corporate shitcoin", but what Vitalik calls CROPS - "censorship and capture resistance, open source, privacy and security".

EIP-1559 is not going anywhere, and Vitalik openly says "don't be sad" if the volume of gas burned will forever stay below the 10% mark. The source of value for Ethereum lies in completely another dimension.

Solana's co-founder, Anatoly Yakovenko, unexpectedly aligned with Buterin on this matter. "You can't focus on fees as the products", he said, calling such an idea "absolutely meaningless". According to Yakovenko, the blockchain network must generate real value for the end users, not control the price of getting into the block.

Vitalik is absolutely right that you can’t focus on fees as the product. That’s absolutely meaningless. You need to pick a product, the thing that brings value to people, and do that really well.

There is lots of technical stuff and philosophy around, but what the missed deadline on the testnet and Vitalik's manifesto reveal is that Ethereum opts for long-term, secure, and even a bit boring stability of the basic layer. Infra doesn't try to flirt with the market through deflationary records.

Why Nvidia Stock Looks Like a Bargain Right Now

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia's shares have stalled, while its business hasn't. Here's the case for buying the dip.

Nvidia (NVDA +2.77%) has made investors a tremendous amount of money over the past several years. But lately, the artificial intelligence chipmaker has been heading in the wrong direction.

Shares have been essentially flat so far in 2026 and have fallen about 22% from their all-time high. That's quite a reversal for a company whose revenue more than doubled in its most recent quarter.

And I think investors are getting an opportunity with Nvidia stock.

Nvidia reports its fiscal third-quarter earnings on Nov. 17, and I'm expecting another blowout performance. The company's financial results and the extraordinary demand for AI computing suggest its growth story has plenty of room to run.

In its fiscal second quarter, which ended July 26, Nvidia generated $96.2 billion in revenue, up 106% from a year earlier. Data center revenue reached $89 billion, an increase of 117%.

Those are remarkable growth rates for a company operating at Nvidia's scale.

Management expects third-quarter revenue of approximately $108 billion, representing another 12% sequential increase at the midpoint. Nvidia also forecasts a non-GAAP gross margin of 74%, suggesting it expects to maintain substantial pricing power despite its enormous sales volume.

And there are good reasons to believe demand remains strong. SK Hynix, a major supplier of high bandwidth memory used in AI accelerators, has indicated that its advanced memory capacity is effectively sold out for years. That matters, because Nvidia's GPUs need that memory to handle increasingly demanding AI workloads.

Meanwhile, Nvidia's Vera Rubin platform is already in full production, with systems being deployed by major cloud providers and AI infrastructure companies.

Customers aren't waiting for AI computing demand to disappear. They're spending billions to secure more capacity.

The latest weakness in Nvidia stock reflects concerns about the enormous cost of AI infrastructure and the possibility that political opposition could slow data center construction.

Those aren't imaginary risks. If companies struggle to obtain electricity or construction permits, some GPU deployments could be delayed.

But there's a difference between a delay in building data centers and a collapse in demand for AI computing. Nvidia has already demonstrated that it can sell enormous quantities of increasingly sophisticated chips while maintaining exceptional margins. Its next earnings report will provide another opportunity to see whether that demand is holding up.

I'm expecting the results to exceed management's already aggressive outlook.

A 24% decline doesn't automatically make any stock cheap, and Nvidia still faces risks from customer spending cuts, competing chips, and restrictions on AI infrastructure development. But with revenue doubling and a new generation of products entering production, I see a stronger business than what the stock's recent performance suggests.

Patrick Sanders has positions in Nvidia. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Nvidia. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Rangers and Portsmouth swerved transfer bullet as Frank Lampard hit the buffers

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Portsmouth head into the international break on an excellent run of form, as John Mousinho's side dare to dream of a place in the Championship eight come May.

Portsmouth, after a difficult start to the campaign where they won just two of their opening eight league games, have bounced back superbly, and instead of looking like relegation candidates, look like an outside shout for a play-off place.

Head coach Mousinho has done a superb job of taking Pompey from the depths of League One and cementing their status in the Championship, as now, the 40-year-old is aiming to take them a level higher.

Recruitment, on the whole, has been exceptional at Fratton Park over recent years despite a lack of funds, as Portsmouth may well have dodged an expensive bullet courtesy of their lack of financial muscle.

Under the ownership of Eisner's, Portsmouth have never spent beyond their means; instead, they have recruited smartly by uncovering undervalued talent across the globe.

Nicolas Schmid joined from Blau Weiss Linz in Austria, Adrian Segecic arrived from Sydney FC in Australia, Rocco Shein from Fredrikstad in Norway, and Marko Milovanovic headed to Fratton Park from Spanish outfit Almeria.

Whilst this summer has seen Pompey splash the cash a bit more than what they have been used to, just six months ago was not a similar story, having lost out on the capture of Surinamese winger Jahnoah Markelo from Swiss outfit FC Zurich to Coventry City.

Mousinho revealed he was interested in signing the winger during the January window in April 2026, but cited his reasoning for not completing the deal as the hefty fee, as Markelo headed to then-division rivals Coventry for around £4.3 million instead.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

Following his move to the CBS Arena, Markelo found himself competing for minutes alongside the likes of Romain Esse, Min-hyeok Yang, Tatsuhiro Sakamoto, and Ephron Mason-Clark, with Brandon Thomas-Asante also offering an option out wide.

Markelo came through the ranks of FC Twente before spending time with Go Ahead Eagles, NK Kustosija Zagreb, NK Celje, and FC Zurich before his move to Coventry, but what looked to be an exciting signing at the time quickly proved to be a confusing one.

The 23-year-old was only included in eight of 17 Championship matchday squads he was available for, playing just a total of 44 minutes across five substitute appearances for the Sky Blues, who would eventually earn promotion to the Premier League via the title, to little impact from Markelo.

Following Coventry's promotion, it was already apparent that Markelo did not have a big role to play under Lampard, and heading up to a division likely meant even fewer opportunities would be afforded to him, and so that proved to be.

Just six months after signing for Coventry for around £4.3 million, a permanent exit was on the cards for the winger, as Scottish giants Rangers reportedly opened talks with the Sky Blues over a deal for Markelo.

However, the Surinamese instead opted to head to the Middle East with United Arab Emirates-based outfit Shabab Al-Ahli, where he has further failed to set the world alight.

Markelo has made just eight appearances for the club to date across all competitions, failing to register a single goal contribution, as right now, it looks like Portsmouth and Rangers have dodged a bullet with this deal.

Do you think Portsmouth and Rangers have dodged a bullet by not signing Markelo, or would things have gone differently had he headed to either of those instead? Share your thoughts below!

With Markelo's lack of impact at either Coventry or Shabab Al-Ahli in the past 11 months or so, it's looking increasingly likely that both Portsmouth and Rangers have gotten away with one here.

Had Pompey had the funds to rival the Sky Blues for Markelo's signature back in January 2026, he may well have headed to Fratton Park instead, though whether he would have had better fortunes under Mousinho remains to be seen.

As it stands, the Surinamese has failed to justify his £4.3 million transfer fee, as Portsmouth and Rangers must both be relieved.

The Phasmophobia community has raised more than $230,000 (around £173,000) for the American Heart Association, more than doubling last year's total.

Developer Kinetic Games announced the final figure for this year's Ghost Hunts for Hearts event on 6th November. The charity drive ran from 26th October to 2nd November, and more than 500 streamers signed up to take part. That's the biggest turnout yet for a creator fundraiser run by Heart Liive, the American Heart Association's streaming initiative.

Last year's event raised a little over $100,000.

Congratulations, Ghost Hunters, you've surpassed the community target and raised over $200,000 for our friends at @Heart_Liive!Your kindness and generosity will not go unrewarded, log in to Phasmophobia before the 15th November to claim the Animated AHA Advocate ID Card &… pic.twitter.com/KFQKaqXNCl

No Matter What Happens to the S&P 500 in November, These 3 Dividend Stocks Belong in Your Portfolio

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A retailer, a spice maker, and a food distributor have raised their dividends for a combined 151 years.

November has a way of making investors feel like they need an opinion about the market. The index is near a record, the AI trade is wobbling, and every headline wants you to decide whether this is the top. Nobody knows, and the portfolios that can thrive during the long stretches of uncertainty share one trait: They include a healthy helping of reliable dividend payers.

The three consumer companies below have paid and raised their dividends through recessions, pandemics, and the bursting of more than one market bubble. Two of them are in the middle of the biggest deals in their histories. All three sell things people keep restocking for their homes in good months and bad.

In June, discount retail giant Target (TGT +0.29%) increased its quarterly dividend by 1.8% to $1.16 per share, making 2026 the 55th straight year it has raised its payout. At the current share price of near $163, that works out to a yield just over 3%, but the dividend still consumes less than half of its earnings, which leaves the company plenty of room to conduct share buybacks and invest in the stores themselves.

The reason Target belongs in retail investors' portfolios is not the yield alone. It is that the company keeps acting like a retailer that expects to be around for another 55 years. This fall, it relaunched Threshold, its house brand for home goods, finished a full remodel of its SoHo store in Manhattan, and promoted a shopping list showing that folks could pull together a Thanksgiving dinner for six in its stores for just $30.

The chain's dividend-hiking streak started in 1971, which means Target raised its payouts through every market the S&P 500 has produced since Richard Nixon was president.

Spice and sauce powerhouse McCormick (MKC -3.32%) is paying a quarterly dividend of $0.48 per share, up from $0.45 per share a year ago, and has raised its dividend annually for 40 consecutive years. From its 2020 peak above $100, the stock has fallen to about $48 today, which pushed its yield up to nearly 4%, the highest it has been in a generation.

What has investors nervous this year -- and what contributed to its year-to-date decline of around 28% in the stock -- is McCormick's pending combination with Unilever's foods business, which the two companies announced in March. That deal would create a company with about $20 billion in annual revenue, with roughly $600 million in expected cost savings, according to Reuters. It's slated to close by mid-2027.

Meanwhile, McCormick's existing business keeps doing what it does. Third-quarter sales rose 17%, gross margin expanded by 190 basis points, and McCormick de Mexico, which it acquired a majority interest in back in January, is already integrated. Spices and sauces are among the least cyclical products in the grocery store -- even in a recession, people are still going to season their food. So McCormick is the kind of business you want dishing out quarterly payments to you while the market argues about chip stocks.

Sysco (SYY +2.15%) has raised its dividend for 57 straight years and now pays $0.55 per share each quarter. Because its streak of hikes has hit the 50-year mark, it has earned the rare title of Dividend King. It's also the most ambitious of these three companies right now. In March, it agreed to buy Restaurant Depot, the cash-and-carry chain where independent restaurant owners go to load up on a wide array of the items they require, from paper towels to chicken. That deal moves Sysco into a higher-margin channel that it previously didn't have.

Sysco already delivers to more than 700,000 customer locations, and every one of those food service locations needs its trucks to show up every week, regardless of whether the S&P 500 is up or down. That is my whole case. The company reported its fiscal 2027 first-quarter results on Nov. 3 and held its full-year guidance steady. Its payout ratio of around 60% is high for a distributor, but Sysco has sustained that for years. Add the Restaurant Depot deal, and you have a reliable dividend grower that is also, for the first time in a while, a growth stock story.

Target gets you the consumer, McCormick gets you the pantry, and Sysco gets you every restaurant in between. None of them are exciting, which is actually the point. To me, the right way to own them is to make them the ballast in a portfolio that also holds the growth names everyone is nervous about this month.

If November goes badly for Wall Street, these three will pay you anyway. If it goes well, you will own three companies that have been raising their dividends since before many of today's investors were born.

Micah Zimmerman has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Sysco and Target. The Motley Fool recommends McCormick. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

The one-hour special airs tonight at 7 p.m. ET/PT on CBS. You can watch the President’s 60 Minutes interview on TV with any basic cable package that includes a local CBS affiliate. Don’t have cable? There are also a few ways to livestream 60 Minutes online.

Building Reliable iOS Apps With AI Agents

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Senior iOS engineer at Uber. Indie app developer. 8+ years of professional experience.

Senior iOS engineer at Uber. Indie app developer. 8+ years of professional experience.

Senior iOS engineer at Uber. Indie app developer. 8+ years of professional experience.

One year ago I wrote Breaking Free from Xcode: Unlock AI's Full Potential in iOS. At that time, getting an agent to build, run and check an iOS app took a pile of MCP servers, VS Code extensions and some patience.

A lot has changed since. Models are much smarter. Xcode ships with agents built in. Claude Code can control the simulator on its own: it builds, launches, taps through screens and checks the result with screenshots.

For prototypes and MVPs, mostly yes. You can describe an idea and get the app “one-shotted”. But if your software has to be as reliable as running water (a phrase I borrow from Uber's mission: "transportation as reliable as running water, everywhere, for everyone"), two principles still matter: design and harness.

This is the biggest shift I've seen. While building a feature, I don't keep in my head which method calls which method, which file it lives in, or how state moves through each type. The agent handles that.

That doesn't mean you can stop reviewing code. Where quality matters, you still read PRs. But your attention moves up a level. You own the design of the whole system, not just one module, so architecture is now the most valuable skill you have.

At the design level you are pretty high in the hierarchy of components, that’s why visualization helps. A diagram with the right amount of detail shows how the pieces fit together, which is hard to see in plain text. The C4 model gives you that visualization at a few zoom levels.

Last year I suggested putting the project architecture in AGENTS.md. I've changed my mind: a dedicated docs/architecture/ folder is a better place. You don't need the entire architecture loaded into the context every time you do something. Instead, AGENTS.md keeps a reference to docs/architecture/context.md, so the agent can retrieve it when on demand. Luis Herrera's deep dive walks through C4 levels on a sample system.

System Context. Your app, its users and the outside systems it talks to: the backend, push notifications, analytics. This is context.md, and it links to the containers.

Containers. Conventional C4 container usually stands for a piece of software that can de deployed, like a micro-service or app. I had to adapt the original idea: a container is a feature, a flow or an SDK module, like Feed, Checkout or Networking. containers.md shows how they connect, explains what each one owns and what it may depend on, and links to components where they exist.

Components. The parts inside a container. Only draw these for complex areas, like a post composer with drafts, uploads and retries, or a sync engine. A stateless FeedService with a single getFeed() call doesn't need a component diagram. C4 itself says you only need the levels "that add value".

Code. No diagrams. Component docs just link to the specific files. The code is the source of truth, and an agent can read it faster than you can draw it.

Think about C4 as a pattern that generalizes an approach to structure progressive complexity of the architecture.

Here's what that looks like for a simple feed app:

Here's the container diagram from containers.md, as Mermaid source and rendered:

Use Mermaid. It's plain text, so it shows up in diffs, GitHub renders it, and agents read it as easily as code.

This is what I call discoverable architecture. The agent doesn't load the whole architecture up front. AGENTS.md points it to context.md, and from there it follows links down only as far as the task needs. Humans onboard the same way.

Documentation that goes stale is worse than none. So let the agent maintain it:

The script needs jq, and don't forget to chmod +x it. The stamp file matters: the docs pass runs once per new set of Swift changes, not after every question you ask.

Models are far more capable now, but they still need guidance. I regularly see agents get confused by strict concurrency and SwiftUI performance. The code compiles and the tests pass but the produced code can crash, that why your hard skills still matter.

I define these priorities in terms of resources (compute and time):

For agent review guardrails, I use skills. The most useful thing in a skill is a pair of bad and good examples. For concurrency, for example, you can show what blocking Swift's cooperative thread pool looks like:

I've caught agents writing the first version more than once and writing a linter could be an overkill or just flaky, so I put it into a skill, the review agent flags concerns when spot bad examples.

Every rule, skill and doc you add competes for the same context window, so load the right context at the right moment. Oleg Efimov explains the cost well in Navigating Claude Code: The Context Window Tax: every token you load is paid for on every turn.

Agents can write the code. What they still can't do is decide what the system should look like, or build the harness that keeps it that way. To recap:

iOS development isn't solved. The hard part has just moved up a level.

Following Patrick Stewart's death, I'm still wrestling with the fact that the beloved actor is no longer with us. At the same time, though, I've also been enjoying the sweet and interesting anecdotes shared by those who've worked with him. With that, I was shocked to read a recently resurfaced story about Wil Wheaton apologizing to Stewart. It turned out the former child actor had some guilt about his behavior on the set of Star Trek: The Next Generation, and I'm not at all surprised to hear how Stewart responded.

The apology apparently came shortly after the end of production on 2002's Star Trek: Nemesis, which Wheaton was originally in before getting cut from the movie. Wheaton wrote in his book Just A Geek that he apologized to Stewart for his cocky behavior on the set of The Next Generation. After admitting he'd "been a dick" during filming, Wheaton wrote that the Picard actor said the following (via Star Trek Fan Group):

Wil, my dear, you were a teenager. We all understood… I wasn't thrilled about working with a child, but working with you was a great pleasure.

It's worth remembering that when Star Trek: The Next Generation first started, Wheaton was the biggest star of the bunch. The majority of audiences knew him through Rob Reiner's 1986 coming-of-age drama, Stand By Me, while Stewart and others were relative unknowns. This, paired with alleged emotional abuse at home, led Wheaton to have an arrogant attitude on set.

Wil Wheaton wouldn't open up about any of that until long after his time working on The Next Generation, and at this point, he's spoken about it at length. As Wheaton previously explained, the TNG cast was there for him when he felt his parents weren't, and he also saw Patrick Stewart as a father figure.

Stewart wrote about Wheaton on the set of TNG in his own memoir, Making It So. While the actor admitted feeling some annoyance at the young actor having such bravado, he ultimately realized it stemmed from his own insecurities as a lead in his breakout television role:

To me, he initially came off as cocky. But as I examined my feelings, I realized that they were not really about Wil or some notion that he should know his place as a juvenile actor — they reflected my own vulnerability. In those first weeks, I wished I had Wil’s confidence.

Wheaton shared a sweet throwback photo of him and Patrick Stewart shortly after news of the latter's death broke. It's great to know that, despite feeling like a "dick" for how he acted, Wheaton has such fond memories of that time and holds such a deep reverence for all of his cast members.

Stewart's death feels like a capstone on a year in which Star Trek's 60th anniversary has felt like a bittersweet celebration. His passing came just on the heels of fans mourning Walter Koenig, who played Pavel Chekov on the original series.

Take those two massive losses and the fact that there are no Star Trek shows in active development, and it's been a bit of a downer year. However, there is a movie in development as well as growing optimism that while Trek is in a valley, it may peak once again in the future. At present, though, I'm content with honoring Patrick Stewart for his contributions to Trek and for his character as human being.

Readers needing Star Trek in their lives can always stream the series on Paramount+, or for free over on Pluto TV. I've been streaming TNG in the wake of Stewart's death and have had a blast watching him in action.

Altcoins have been bleeding more than Bitcoin [BTC], even though the latter is the main driver of the crypto market’s sentiment. In total, altcoins have lost more than $200 billion in capitalization, implying that the euphoria around them was short-lived.

Deducing Binance’s market, over 70% of the altcoins broke below the 200-day moving average (MA). Here, it’s worth noting that this share is quickly approaching the 80% mark. For instance, at press time, Solana [SOL] was trading at $89 with the MA positioned at $97.76.

The broader altcoin market’s weakness has been accelerated by macroeconomic uncertainty. Especially after President Trump’s Republicans lost control of the two chambers of the U.S Congress.

Additionally, the Federal Reserve, European Central Bank, and Bank of England raised interest rates this week. This move discourages borrowing capital, which would be used to invest in risk-on assets like alts.

The discrepancy between the current altcoin performance and that of mid-August is evidence of shifting market bias. Between August and September, the cap of altcoins rose by $320 billion. However, since then, it has shed 62.5% of what it gained.

Solana slid after the midterm elections’ outcome, exerting pressure on the altcoin. At press time, SOL was 9.75% away from its 200-day MA. This reading indicated that bears were in control of the market.

Even so, traders are betting on the growing RWA market to help mitigate the downside risk. As such, SOL could be an outlier when the general altcoin market is weak. Bitcoin would also need to be bullish for more confluence though.

PHP .user.ini can make auto_prepend_file a persistence path

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

PHP's per-directory .user.ini files are configuration, but they can influence which code runs before a requested script. The auto_prepend_file directive names a file that PHP parses before the main file, as if it had been loaded with require. If an application permits an attacker to create or modify a .user.ini file in the document tree, a temporary upload or template-write flaw can become a persistent execution path.

The manual says .user.ini processing is available for the CGI/FastCGI SAPI. PHP scans from the requested script's directory upward to the document root, and only settings in the allowed modes are recognized. auto_prepend_file is a per-directory setting. The file is resolved with include_path, which adds another configuration value to the review.

An uploaded file is usually not executed directly. The problem begins when a user can place content in a directory that PHP scans for .user.ini, or when a deployment process writes generated files beside application code. The next request in that directory can inherit settings that the application did not intend.

The cache matters. user_ini.cache_ttl controls how often the per-directory configuration is reread. A response to an incident may remove a malicious file while a running FastCGI worker retains the previous setting for some period. Treat the cache as part of containment, not as a reason to assume the file is harmless.

The risk is not limited to a file named shell.php. A prepended file can contain initialization logic, logging hooks, or code that changes the application's runtime. Reviewers need to look at who can write any path that may be treated as configuration and at what code that configuration can include.

Inventory all document roots and every writable directory below them. Include uploads, cache, generated thumbnails, plugin directories, and deployment release folders that are web-accessible. Search for .user.ini files and for auto_prepend_file or auto_append_file references in deployment templates. Check for symlinks and path normalization differences between the web server and PHP.

Inspect the effective PHP-FPM configuration for user_ini.filename, user_ini.cache_ttl, open_basedir, include_path, and allow_url_include. Confirm whether web-server location rules deny requests to dot files and whether the denial covers every virtual host and alias. An application-level 404 is not a filesystem restriction if PHP still scans the directory.

Review the deployment user's write permissions. A release process that can replace application code is highly privileged; it should not also accept untrusted filenames or extract archives into the live document root. Separate writable data from executable code, and keep the web process unable to create PHP files where it can also write configuration.

If per-directory configuration is not required, set user_ini.filename to an empty value and deploy the decision through the PHP-FPM pool configuration. Where it is required, deny direct access to .user.ini, set a short cache lifetime appropriate to the deployment, and make the file immutable by the web user. Use open_basedir to bound file access, and do not allow remote inclusion unless the application has a documented and reviewed need.

Set a trusted include_path and disable URL wrappers for application files. Protect the FastCGI socket and pool configuration from unprivileged users. A user who can change the pool configuration can bypass many application-level controls.

In a staging copy, create a harmless .user.ini that points auto_prepend_file at a test file, then request a script in that directory. Confirm that the test file is included and record the cache delay. Remove the file and repeat the request after the cache expires. Try the same path through the public web server, an alias, and any alternate hostname.

Review application logs for changes to .user.ini, unexpected include failures, and requests for dot files. Correlate those events with upload and deployment activity. The useful control is a path boundary: untrusted data must not be able to change which code PHP loads before the application starts.

Preserve a missing count when drafting an abstract results table

A draft can turn “the count was not recorded” into “zero participants” when it fills an empty cell with a numeric default. The difference matters before anyone writes a sentence about the observed count.

Here is a synthetic table export. An empty cell means the count is unavailable in this export. A literal zero means the table recorded zero. The script keeps those cases separate.

Group A can keep the literal zero from the export. Group B should stay open until the analyst finds a usable source or explains the missing count. How to find broken #anchor links, the ones every link checker says are fine

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You rename a heading in your docs from "Install" to "Installation". Your static site generator dutifully changes the heading's id from #install to #installation. Every link that pointed to /docs/setup#install now lands at the top of a long page instead of the section the reader needed.

Your link checker reports no problems. The page returns 200. Technically, nothing is broken.

Fragment links (the #something part of a URL) are one of the quietest kinds of link rot. The server never sees the fragment, so no HTTP check can catch it. Here's how to find them yourself.

The fragment refers to an element with a matching id (or, in older HTML, an ). Those ids usually come from one of three places:

Heading-based ids are the main culprit. Every copy edit to a heading is a potential broken link, and the people editing headings are rarely thinking about who links to them.

We'll do this against the built HTML output, which is what readers actually get, rather than the Markdown source.

This uses Node with cheerio for HTML parsing (npm install cheerio). Point it at your build directory.

Because it exits non-zero when it finds problems, you can drop it straight into CI after your build step.

Trailing slashes and clean URLs. The urlPathFor function assumes about/index.html is served at /about/ and about.html at /about. If your host does something different, adjust the mapping. This is the part most likely to need tweaking.

JavaScript-rendered ids. If ids are added by client-side code, they won't be in the built HTML. You'd need a headless browser to render pages first. For most docs sites, ids are in the static output.

Case sensitivity. Fragments are case-sensitive in HTML. #Install and #install are different. The script treats them that way, which matches what browsers do.

Encoded characters. Headings with non-English characters produce encoded ids. The script decodes the fragment before comparing; check a few results if your docs are multilingual.

When you find a broken fragment, you have two options.

Update the link to the new id. Fine for links inside your own site, which you control.

Keep the old id alive for links you don't control: other sites, old blog posts, Stack Overflow answers pointing to your docs. Add an empty anchor with the old id next to the new heading:

Many Markdown tools also let you set explicit heading ids, for example ## Installation {#installation}. Using explicit ids for important headings means copy edits no longer change URLs.

The most effective fix isn't the script, it's the policy: treat heading ids in docs as public URLs. Once a section has been linked to, its id is part of your API, and changing it deserves the same care as changing a page URL.

Fragment checks are one of the details we care about at linkbruno.com, because a link that lands in the wrong place wastes the reader's time just as surely as a 404.

Do you check fragment links on your site today, or have you found a tool that handles them well? I'd love to hear how others deal with heading renames.

Postmortem: the deploy deleted last week's JavaScript bundles, and cached pages kept asking for them

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This is a composite postmortem: the details are illustrative, assembled from a failure pattern that turns up in many front-end deploy pipelines. No real company or figures are described.

After a routine front-end deploy, a portion of visitors saw blank pages or pages without styles for a period after each release. The cause: the deploy deleted the previous build's hashed JavaScript and CSS files, while HTML pages cached at the CDN and in browsers still referenced them. Those references returned 404s.

The site's link checker reported zero broken links throughout, because it only checked links, never

Mimikara 耳から - Display English sub-titles when they are spoken

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I'v watched many days worth of anime over my life and learnt surprisingly little Japanese, despite always chosing original language audio. I was hoping to learn more Japanese, but incidentally, without effort and think it may be because as we read thee English subtitles left to right - the translation doesn't correspond to the audio. (I know translation doesn't mean one-to-one subtitle to spoken word, still, over time one would learn, I hope, corresponding Japanese words for their English translations). For example, Luffy's famous line:

I always thought "Kaizoku-ō" meant King (because it sounds like German for emperorer: "Kaiser"), but actually it means Pirate!

So I have just started a project: mimikara, which only shows the English subtitle at the exact time the audio is spoken

It works by turning a Japanese and an English subtitle file into .ass subtitles where each English word fades in at the moment its Japanese counterpart is spoken, by pairing the cues by time, tokenising the Japanese with UniDic, asking an LLM which English word maps to which Japanese word, timing those words by mora count or forced alignment, and rendering the line with colour and opacity changes only so the English still reads naturally left to right.
